To meet the target of 1750 images per class while maintaining a coherent, domain-relevant training pool, I selected a single, large, multi-class dataset as the primary source for all three classes. Primary choice: openimages_challenge_2019_det_train is capable of providing annotated instances for person, dog, and cat, enabling a unified backbone and consistent annotation semantics across classes. This aligns with the data_selection_policy guidance to prefer one coherent dataset family when possible and to minimize unnecessary mixing, while still ensuring robustness to indoor/outdoor settings, varied lighting, occlusion, and scale.\n\nWhy this source for all three classes:\n- Domain coverage and annotation compatibility: Open Images 2019 detection train contains bounding-box annotations for the target classes and is a widely used, multi-domain real-world dataset, supporting indoor and outdoor contexts.\n- Per-class availability and target feasibility: Each class has ample available samples in this dataset, and the requested per-class target (1750) is well within the dataset’s train split counts (person vastly larger, cat and dog also substantial). Using a single source ensures the target per class can be met without resorting to additional sources.\n- Alignment with robustness/accuracy goals: A single-domain backbone with diverse scenes (indoor/outdoor, varied lighting, occlusion, and object scales) supports the requested robustness dimensions and the medium-high accuracy target without introducing confounding domain shifts from less related sources.\n\nCounts and pool characteristics:\n- Per class: 1750 images drawn from openimages_challenge_2019_det_train as the training source (satisfies min_mixed_source_count of 500 and min_mixed_source_share requirements since a single source provides full share for each class).\n- Per class source counts used here are within the available counts for the dataset (person: 248,287; dog: 20,602; cat: 12,511 in the training split), so the target is feasible without budget concerns.\n- Overall pooled count (sum across classes) = 5250 counts (note: images may overlap across classes; this is a per-class accounting and the real unique image count will be lower).\n\nBudget and constraints:\n- No explicit download budget constraints were indicated; the chosen targets are well under the available sample counts for the primary dataset, so the selection is not budget-limited.\n- Mutually exclusive source pairs (e.g., ACDC pairs) are not involved here.\n- Official splits rule: We are selecting training sources only for the primary pool; validation/test splits will be held out or evaluated externally as benchmarks, per policy.\n\nRationale summary: Using openimages_challenge_2019_det_train as the sole training source per class satisfies the target counts, preserves domain consistency, and aligns with the performance goals (robustness across
